A long-sleeved V-neck cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a bold flat-colour illustration with thick black outlines, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a steam enthusiast. The print is cut to the T-shirt's shape, so the shirt itself looks like the outfit being worn, with the sleeves as the outfit's sleeves. The outfit's front panels run straight down the shirt's front with its shoulders sitting on the shirt's shoulder seams, and its sleeves are printed along the shirt's sleeves to cuffs at the sleeve hems, showing an engine driver's jacket. Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the same outfit looks from behind. The outfit's own cloth covers the whole shirt as one continuous print, so shoulders, sleeves and body are all in the outfit's colours. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. The printed top garment continues down the shirt to its straight hem, with at most a belt or sash at the waist. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The outfit is stretched to the shirt's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. A long-sleeved shirt is used so the outfit's long sleeves fit exactly, printed full length to the shirt's wrist hems. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. The picture is only the garment, its cloth, seams, buttons and trims laid flat across the whole shirt like a sewing pattern. It also shows printed cuffs at the sleeve hems. It is a trompe-l'oeil T-shirt, a plain cotton shirt pretending to be the outfit. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem and the headwear's emblem or crest worked into the chest, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.
A long-sleeved V-neck cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a comic-style inked illustration print, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a Tour de France spectator. The print is cut to the T-shirt's shape, so the shirt itself looks like the outfit being worn, with the sleeves as the outfit's sleeves. The outfit's front panels run straight down the shirt's front with its shoulders sitting on the shirt's shoulder seams, and its sleeves are printed along the shirt's sleeves to cuffs at the sleeve hems, showing a polka-dot costume and a flag. Turn the shirt around and the back carries the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge with nothing left plain. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. The shirt is an ordinary T-shirt ending in a straight bottom hem, and the garment printed on it runs straight down the whole shirt to that hem. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the shirt is outfit cloth. The outfit is stretched to the shirt's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. The printed collar and lapels line up with the shirt's actual neckline. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem, the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit and the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

Every panel of the shirt, front, shoulders and sleeves, is a panel of the outfit, showing a blazer with a club patch. The back of the shirt is printed just as completely, edge to edge, with the back of the same outfit, its back collar, shoulder blades and the backs of both sleeves. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. It is an ordinary T-shirt in shape, with a straight open hem and sleeves cut to match the outfit's sleeves. The shirt is an ordinary T-shirt ending in a straight bottom hem, and the garment printed on it runs straight down the whole shirt to that hem. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the shirt is outfit cloth. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided shirt shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. The shirt is solid opaque cotton and every texture and sheen is a printed picture. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. It also shows printed cuffs at the sleeve hems. It is a flat print, not a framed picture, not a small centred graphic, not a standing figure and not a drawing of a garment on a hanger. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem and the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. It also shows a printed collar continuing the outfit up to the neckline. The print wraps around the sides so the outfit seems to continue around the body. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit, its pockets, flaps and trims and the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

The shirt is solid opaque cotton and every texture and sheen is a printed picture. The picture is only the garment, its cloth, seams, buttons and trims laid flat across the whole shirt like a sewing pattern. It also shows printed cuffs and wrists at the sleeve hems. It is the kind of novelty tee that tricks the eye from across a room. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces, its pockets, flaps and trims and the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

The shirt is an ordinary T-shirt ending in a straight bottom hem, and the garment printed on it runs straight down the whole shirt to that hem. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The outfit is laid out on the shirt's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. It also shows printed gloves emerging at the sleeve hems. It is a trompe-l'oeil T-shirt, a plain cotton shirt pretending to be the outfit. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces, the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit and the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

The outfit's front panels run straight down the shirt's front with its shoulders sitting on the shirt's shoulder seams, and its sleeves are printed along the shirt's sleeves to cuffs at the sleeve hems, showing an engine driver's jacket. The back of the shirt is printed just as completely, edge to edge, with the back of the same outfit, its back collar, shoulder blades and the backs of both sleeves. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. The shirt is an ordinary T-shirt ending in a straight bottom hem, and the garment printed on it runs straight down the whole shirt to that hem. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the shirt is outfit cloth. The outfit is laid out on the shirt's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. A long-sleeved shirt is used so the outfit's long sleeves fit exactly, printed full length to the shirt's wrist hems. The shirt is solid opaque cotton and every texture and sheen is a printed picture. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. It also shows a printed collar around the real neckline. The print wraps around the sides so the outfit seems to continue around the body. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem and its pockets, flaps and trims, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. It also shows printed gloves emerging at the sleeve hems. The print wraps around the sides so the outfit seems to continue around the body. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem, the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve and the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

The uniform fills the whole straight-sided shirt shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The shirt is a short-sleeved T-shirt, and the outfit's sleeves are printed as short sleeves ending exactly at the shirt's sleeve hems. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. The picture is only the garment, its cloth, seams, buttons and trims laid flat across the whole shirt like a sewing pattern. Under every lace, net, mesh or see-through area the print shows __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__, as a printed illusion on solid cotton. It also shows printed cuffs at the sleeve hems. Tiny printed shadows and fold lines suggest the garment without any real depth. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve, the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces and the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

The print wraps all the way round, the back of the shirt shows the rear of the outfit from the back collar to the hem and along both sleeves. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. Only the upper garment is printed, running all the way down to the shirt's straight hem. The print is cut exactly to the shirt, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided shirt shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The shirt is cut sleeveless, with armholes where the outfit's sleeves would start. All textures, transparencies and sheens are printed patterns on solid cotton. Only the outfit's fabric, trims and fastenings are pictured, spread across the entire shirt as a continuous pattern. It also shows a printed collar continuing the outfit up to the neckline and printed cuffs where the sleeves end. It is a flat print, not a framed picture, not a small centred graphic, not a standing figure and not a drawing of a garment on a hanger. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve, its pockets, flaps and trims and the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

It is a flat print, not a framed picture, not a small centred graphic, not a standing figure and not a drawing of a garment on a hanger. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces and the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.
